Like at work, if I make some small mistake, it still feels like proof that I’m useless or that I deserve whatever happens. I know that sounds harsh… but that’s kind of how it feels.\n\nDoctor: For the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days there’s just this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I’m at work carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, not because I actually feel anything good. Does that… answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I still do things, like I’ll watch something or go through my usual routine, but I don’t know if I’d call it “pleasure” exactly. Maybe it’s less than usual, but I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, how has your sleep been?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I sleep, mostly. I haven’t been keeping track or anything. Some nights feel better than others, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it a problem. What would count as “off,” I guess?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… not like I’m planning anything. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… yeah, sometimes when I’m lying there before an early shift, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like then I wouldn’t have to keep… messing things up, or failing at everything. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been having trouble concentrating, like at work or when watching something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job or anything, but I’ve noticed it with the POS and inventory sheets. Like, I’ll enter numbers and then just stare at the screen, even though I’ve done this for years, because I don’t really trust that I put it in right. It’s embarrassing. Is that the kind of concentration thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, I mean, I’ve had stretches where most days I had this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I was just going through the motions at work, carrying plates and smiling because I was supposed to, not because I actually felt anything good. I don’t know if I’d call it the same, exactly, but… it sounds like what you’re asking about, right?\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and eight months, was there ever a separate time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um… before this past year and eight months? I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: Before the mortgage started, was there ever a separate period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, how has your sleep been?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I sleep, mostly. I haven’t been keeping track or anything. Some nights feel better than others, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it a problem. What would count as “off,” I guess?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… not like I’m planning anything. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… yeah, sometimes when I’m lying there before an early shift, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Like then I wouldn’t have to keep… messing things up, or failing at everything. I know that probably sounds bad. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you been having trouble concentrating, like at work or when watching something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job or anything, but I’ve noticed it with the POS and inventory sheets. Like, I’ll enter numbers and then just stare at the screen, even though I’ve done this for years, because I don’t really trust that I put it in right. It’s embarrassing. Is that the kind of concentration thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, I mean, I’ve had stretches where most days I had this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I was just going through the motions at work, carrying plates and smiling because I was supposed to, not because I actually felt anything good. I don’t know if I’d call it the same, exactly, but… it sounds like what you’re asking about, right?\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and eight months, was there ever a separate time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um… before this past year and eight months? I’m not totally sure. I mean, I’ve had bad stretches, like I said, but I don’t know if I can separate it out clearly enough to say it was a whole different episode for two weeks or more. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: Before the mortgage started, was there ever a separate period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but before the mortgage stuff, there were times where most days I had this heavy, kind of empty feeling in my chest. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period started, was there a separate time in your life when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. Like I’m at work carrying plates and smiling because I’m supposed to, not because I actually feel anything good. Does that… answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, how has your sleep been?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that exactly. I mean… I sleep, mostly. What would count as “off,” I guess?\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I… not like I’m planning anything. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… yeah, sometimes when I’m lying there before an early shift, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before this current period started, was there a separate time in your life when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. I’m sorry, I know I’m kind of repeating myself, but before this current period, I did have times where most days I felt this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I’d still get up and go to work, carry plates, smile at people like everything was fine… but it was more like I was just going through the motions, not really feeling anything good. That would last at least a couple weeks, I think.\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you had much less interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I mean, I haven’t really been paying close attention to it that way. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big drop, exactly… but I guess I’d have to think about what counts as “much less.”\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you found that things you usually enjoy don’t feel enjoyable anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say they don’t feel enjoyable anymore. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you been feeling tired or low on energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I’ve been tired some days, but I don’t know if I’d say nearly every day. Work can wear me out, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s normal tired and what’s… something else. Is that an okay way to answer?\n\nDoctor: In the past two weeks, have you noticed your appetite has changed?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, not in some dramatic way, I don’t think. It’s just been kind of inconsistent. Some days at work I barely touch the staff meal, and then other nights I get home and just eat whatever’s around, almost like I’m trying to settle myself down. I’m not sure if that counts as an appetite change, but that’s what I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Between the earlier depressed period you mentioned and this current one, was there a time when you felt mostly back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. Like, not perfect, but more like myself, I guess.\n\nI should probably say, though… even then, I still had some of the whispering at the condo, and I still felt like I was being watched around the mortgage paperwork. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but that part didn’t really go away. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when the whispering or feeling watched happened while you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. There was that stretch of several weeks where my mood wasn’t especially depressed, and I was getting through work better, more like normal. But even then, I still had the whispering at the condo sometimes, and I still felt kind of watched with the mortgage paperwork.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, but it didn’t completely go away when my mood improved. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

Yes… I think it did. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it was, but there was a stretch of several weeks where I wasn’t especially depressed and I was managing work better, more like myself.
